On the critical traces, answer access provides little benefit, and first-error localization barely improves. That is the central empirical point: when the answer is wrong, monitors behave more like outcome checkers; when the answer is right but the process is flawed, their actual argument-verification ability is much weaker.

A useful background number from the paper is that 65% of traces end with the correct answer, yet some of those traces are still invalid. The authors explicitly connect this to the structure of reward hacking: acceptable output does not imply acceptable process. If monitoring relies on answer visibility, these cases will be systematically missed.

The most informative comparisons are not between monitor models, but between conditions: blind vs unverified vs certified vs commit-then-reveal. Those deltas support the paper’s main claim that answer access changes what is being measured.4a:T74e,The benchmark, VoiceLongMemEval, is built around a strict invariant: every item must be solvable from paralinguistic metadata and unsolvable from text alone. To enforce that, each user turn is annotated with five layers: one of 12 everyday emotion labels, a prosody tuple covering rate, pitch, loudness, pauses, and emphasized words, one of five voice-event types, pragmatic flags such as sarcasm and uncertainty, and a free-text delivery description. A good design choice is that the free-text description is constrained to acoustic facts only. Interpretive emotion words are banned, so the task does not collapse into keyword matching.

Construction proceeds through annotation, evidence authoring and hardening, question generation, and speech synthesis. A small number of evidence sessions are embedded into roughly 100k-token conversation histories together with topic-screened filler sessions and emotive but answer-free distractors. Needle positions are stratified over early, middle, and late parts of the history, and multi-evidence arcs are distributed across sessions. This makes the benchmark closer to long-horizon retrieval under realistic clutter rather than a short-context classification task.

The most important engineering contribution is the three-stage adversarial gate against lexical leakage. G1 requires the blind transcript-only render to be unsolvable. G2 checks that the descriptive render with paralinguistic metadata is solvable. G3 applies static checks for interpretive terms, stock phrases, and valence-presupposing wording. The authors iterate with a 7B judge-adversary and then gate with Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct-AWQ, requiring two consecutive clean runs on a frozen file. That is a sensible response to the main failure mode of this benchmark family: if the text itself leaks the answer, the task no longer measures paralinguistic memory.4b:T6cb,The benchmark contains 523 questions in total, with a 202-item adversarially gated core, built from 326 paralinguistically annotated evidence sessions embedded in about 100k-token histories. The taxonomy covers six task types: affect recall, affective preference, affect update, cross-session affect, temporal-affective reasoning, and prosody-disambiguated interpretation, plus abstention variants.

Eight models are evaluated, including three proprietary and five open-weight systems. The main result is a consistent affect gap: access to paralinguistic information improves performance by +0.09 to +0.38 across all systems, with p < 0.001. Component ablations show that explicit emotion tags are the strongest signal. The paper also argues that chain-of-thought cannot compensate for missing paralinguistic context. In controlled counterfactual analysis, the net gain attributable strictly to metadata content is about +0.067.

The benchmark-construction diagnostics are also useful. A 72B blind adversary still solved 8 items, or 7.5%, after earlier 7B gating. The authors categorize leakage into five mechanisms: pragmatic-prior leakage, outcome tells, gold-matches-prior, default-recovery priors, and A/B gifts. Later batches reportedly removed authoring-time leaks. That part is benchmark engineering, but it is informative because it exposes common ways long-context evaluations accidentally become text-solvable.

From a pretraining perspective, the most relevant findings are not leaderboard differences. The important takeaways are that transcript-only representations systematically lose memory-relevant information, and that structured paralinguistic metadata is a stronger and more reliable signal than extra reasoning tokens.4c:T7cb,There are three main design choices. First, the dataset is built from active but less canonical scientific literature rather than textbook equations. The final benchmark contains 118 problems, 291 candidate laws, 381 papers, and about 8M real data points across six disciplines. It also includes both single-group and multi-group settings. In the multi-group case, models must infer a shared functional form while allowing some parameters to vary across groups, which is closer to real scientific modeling than fitting one global curve.

Second, evaluation is split into two complementary settings. In SciLaws-Real, the model receives fixed real observations and must propose a closed-form law. Published formulas are treated as references, not mandatory targets. Proposed laws are judged along two separate axes: held-out predictive fit and source-grounded scientific validity. This is an important distinction. A formula can fit well numerically and still be scientifically wrong if it violates domain constraints, behaves badly in the relevant regime, or introduces unsupported mechanisms.

Third, SciLaws-Parallel converts the same scientific problem into an active experimental environment. The hidden law is not the published one; it is a newly synthesized structural variant derived from the published form, with coefficients and residual noise calibrated to the corresponding real records. Models start without observations, choose measurements under a fixed budget, and try to recover the hidden law. This setup preserves scientific context while making it possible to test novel-structure recovery rather than literature recall.

Operationally, the authors evaluate nine frontier LLMs under a unified ReAct-style agent framework, with a Python sandbox and, for the parallel setting, a fixed-budget experimentation interface. Each trial allows up to 30 interaction turns, so the benchmark measures multi-step reasoning and tool-mediated exploration rather than one-shot text generation.4d:T5e1,The main empirical findings are threefold. First, models struggle to balance predictive accuracy and scientific validity. The paper gives a nuclear-physics example where the best-fitting formula introduces a nonexistent resonance peak, so it fails the validity check despite good numerical fit. This shows why held-out error alone is not enough.

Second, models are much better at reproducing known laws than at discovering new structure. A cold-recall audit indicates that SciLaws-Bench is less memorized than textbook-style benchmarks. Under weaker recall, models do explore beyond published formulas more often, but successful recovery of genuinely novel structure remains rare. Exploration is present; robust structural induction is not.

Third, models can generate stronger candidates than they can reliably select. Best-of-N search consistently exposes better formulas, but self-selection captures only a small fraction of that gain. This is an important result because it shifts part of the bottleneck from proposal generation to ranking, verification, and candidate selection.

In terms of scale, the benchmark covers 118 problems, 291 candidate laws, 381 papers, and roughly 8M real data points, and evaluates nine frontier models including GPT-5.5, Claude Opus 4.8, Gemini 3.5 Flash, and DeepSeek-V4 Pro. The full paper contains the detailed tables, but the headline conclusion is clear: current LLMs show signs of scientific-law discovery ability, yet the capability is still far from reliable.4e:T403,Start with the introduction and Figure 1 to understand why the benchmark is split into SciLaws-Real and SciLaws-Parallel. Then read the benchmark construction section carefully: the paper-selection criteria, the cold-recall audit, and the role of single-group versus multi-group tasks are all central. After that, focus on the evaluation protocol, especially how predictive fit is separated from scientific validity and how the hidden-law synthesis in the parallel world avoids collapsing into a purely synthetic toy setup.

In the results, three analyses deserve priority: cases where fit and validity disagree, the relationship between recall and novel-structure recovery, and the gap between best-of-N generation and model self-selection. Those sections are enough for a useful skim in about 35 to 50 minutes. If reading more deeply, pay attention to how stable the validity rubric is, whether the synthesized structural variants introduce distribution shift, and how much the fixed interaction budget affects model ranking.4f:T8e8,方法上，这篇文章做得比较扎实，重点不在模型创新，而在协议设计和误差定位。

第一，作者在两个域上统一了评测协议。数学部分使用 MathNet-Retrieve：500 个 query，对 117,088 条完整语料全库排序，不做 shortlist。每个 query 都有唯一 gold source problem，同时 benchmark 还人为放入一个 lexical 相近但结构不同的 near-miss。作者同时报告 strict Hit@k（只认 gold）和 lenient Hit@k（gold 或 near-miss 都算），这个差值正好用来量化“模型是不是被表面相似项吸走了”。

第二，trajectory 部分没有沿用原 benchmark 的 pooled relevance，而是把 336 条 ALFWorld 轨迹全部按 task type 重新穷举标注，并进一步定义两层 disguise：定义(i)要求同 task type 但 target object 不同；定义(ii)再额外要求 receptacle 也不同，尽量去掉最容易驱动 lexical similarity 的 token。这里的设计很关键，因为它把“程序相同”与“对象词相同”拆开了。

第三，作者专门研究 lexical control 的符号翻转。他们引入一个便宜的 lexical reranker，观察它在两个域里的效果方向：在数学里它会伤害结构检索，因为它把 near-miss 顶上去；在轨迹里它反而能提升结果，因为 benchmark 构造仍保留了某些表面线索。随后又做 token-class ablation，去定位究竟是哪些 token 类别在驱动这个现象。

第四，作者分析了 LLM reranking 的 judge dependence。不是只看“加了 reranker 后有没有提升”，而是换三个独立训练的 judge，看恢复方向是否稳定、效应量是否可迁移。结论是方向大体一致，但幅度、tier profile、甚至哪个 judge 更强，都会随域和 query style 改变。这说明 reranker/judge 的结论可移植性很差。

第五，文章还做了 contamination probe 和 downstream null analysis。前者检查 reranking 增益是否集中在知名竞赛题上；后者在一个刻意构造的坏检索条件下比较 oracle retrieval 与 adversarial retrieval，发现下游几乎无差异，并进一步把原因定位到答案截断与评测解析问题，而不是 retrieval 真没作用。这个部分虽然偏评测卫生，但很有现实意义。50:T873,最关键的结果在数学检索。重 disguise 条件下，两个 production embedders 的 strict Hit@1 都是 0.0%，bootstrap 95% CI 也是 [0.0, 0.0]。但正确答案通常并没有消失，而是“在 top-10 里几乎总能找到”。这说明问题不是召回不到，而是排序头部被 lexical near-miss 占据。作者进一步统计 miss 的构成：在 95.2% 到 99.8% 的 miss 中，排在正确答案前面的候选在词面上比 gold 更接近 query。这基本把失败机制钉死在 surface-form bias 上。

轨迹检索的结果更微妙。只要 gold 定义排除了 query 中的 literal object 和 receptacle token，embedding retrieval 就会掉到 chance 以下。这个现象比“性能下降”更强，因为它说明模型不仅没抓住结构，反而系统性偏向错误的表面匹配项。

lexical reranker 的实验是全文最有信息量的对照之一：同一个 lexical control，在数学上伤害 retrieval，在 trajectory 上提升 retrieval。作者通过 token-class ablation 说明，这不是偶然噪声，而是 benchmark 中哪些 token 被保留、哪些被 disguise 的结果。

LLM reranking 部分，作者发现三个 judge 在“是否有恢复趋势”上方向一致，但效应量并不稳定；其中一个 judge 在同一域内跨 query style 时，增益会下降超过三分之一。也就是说，judge 排名本身都不稳。

污染分析里，reranking 的收益更多集中在知名竞赛题；在 6 个 judge-by-candidate cell 中，只有 1 个在 bootstrap 下能自信拒绝零效应，其余更多是方向一致但统计强度不足。这个表述比较克制，没有把 contamination 说得过头。

下游实验里，作者构造了 oracle retrieval 与 adversarial retrieval 的配对比较，结果几乎无差异。进一步做 complete-answers-only 分析后，发现原先 headline 的 69.5% zero-shot accuracy 很大程度上只是 truncation proxy，导致 retrieval 实际可发挥的 headroom 接近于零。另一个很实用的结果是，他们记录了 8 个 evaluation-integrity incident，其中多数与 silent truncation 被误解析为有效输出有关。51:T813,The contribution is in protocol design and failure localization, not in proposing a new retriever.

First, the authors enforce a shared evaluation protocol across two domains. In mathematics, they use 500 queries from MathNet-Retrieve against the full 117,088-item MathNet corpus, with no shortlist. Each query has exactly one gold source problem, plus a planted lexical near-miss that is structurally different. They report both strict Hit@k, which counts only the gold, and lenient Hit@k, which also counts the near-miss. The gap between the two is a direct measure of surface-form attraction.

Second, for ALFWorld trajectories they do not rely on pooled relevance from the source benchmark. Instead, they exhaustively label all 336 corpus items by task type and define two disguise regimes: same task type but different target object, and then same task type with both object and receptacle changed. This is important because it separates procedural similarity from literal token overlap.

Third, they study a lexical-control sign flip. A simple lexical reranker is applied in both domains. In math it hurts structural retrieval by promoting the planted near-miss; in trajectories it helps because the benchmark still preserves some surface cues. Token-class ablations are then used to identify which token categories drive the effect.

Fourth, they analyze judge dependence for LLM reranking. Instead of reporting a single reranking gain, they compare three independently trained judges and ask whether the recovery direction, effect size, and judge ranking are stable across domains and query styles. The answer is largely no for effect size portability.

Fifth, they add a contamination probe and a downstream null analysis. The contamination probe checks whether reranking gains concentrate on well-known competition problems. The downstream analysis compares oracle and adversarial retrieval under a deliberately bad retrieval condition and then traces the null result to truncation and evaluation artifacts rather than to retrieval being inherently useless.52:T7f3,The strongest result is in mathematics. Under the heaviest disguise tier, strict Hit@1 is 0.0% for both production embedders, with bootstrap 95% confidence intervals of [0.0, 0.0]. But the gold item is usually still retrievable: it is almost always somewhere in the top 10. So the failure is not recall collapse; it is head-of-list ranking being captured by lexical near-misses. The mechanism is quantified directly: in 95.2% to 99.8% of misses, the item ranked above the gold is more lexically similar to the query than the correct answer.

The trajectory results are subtler but arguably more revealing. Once the gold definition excludes the literal object and receptacle tokens from the query, embedding retrieval falls below chance. That is stronger than a simple degradation result: it indicates systematic attraction toward the wrong surface match.

The lexical reranker experiment is one of the most informative controls in the paper. The same lexical signal damages retrieval in mathematics and improves it in trajectories. Token-class ablations support the claim that this is driven by benchmark construction, not by noise.

For LLM reranking, the recovery direction replicates across three judges, but the effect size does not transport cleanly. One judge loses more than a third of its gains across query styles within the same domain. So even judge ranking is unstable.

In the contamination probe, reranking gains are concentrated on well-known competitions. Out of six judge-by-candidate cells, only one is confidently non-null under bootstrap resampling, while the others are directionally consistent but weaker statistically.

In the downstream study, oracle and adversarial retrieval are effectively indistinguishable. A complete-answers-only analysis then shows that the headline 69.5% zero-shot accuracy is largely a truncation proxy, leaving almost no effective headroom for retrieval to matter. The paper also documents eight evaluation-integrity incidents, most involving silent truncation parsed as valid output.53:T4cf,ExBind is built around a representation-independent latent binding space and deterministic compilation/evaluation:
1) Sample a latent binding instance containing entities, relations, hierarchy, attributes, ordered roles (e.g., directed edge endpoints), and a unique canonical target.
2) Compile the same latent instance into heterogeneous executable surfaces (SVG, DOM, canvas, tree, graph, table), each with its own surface-specific executable identifiers.
3) The model outputs only the reference required by the environment. The evaluator maps the prediction back into the latent space deterministically, then reports:
- validity (is the output a valid candidate under the declared vocabulary/format?)
- exact binding correctness (does it match the gold target?)
- which structural constraints are violated; for families with an ordered constraint decomposition, a concise first-violation diagnosis.
4) The benchmark explicitly distinguishes outcome stability from diagnostic-type stability via candidate-order perturbations.
The dataset is intentionally small to preserve identifiability: unique gold targets, deterministic mappings, structural-oracle recovery, and shortcut checks are prioritized over population-scale ranking.54:T568,The authors first run controlled ablations on a compact 13M-example dataset, then scale the resulting recipe to Pixel Linguist II trained on 280M examples. The method has four main components. First, it uses a native-resolution ViT-style encoder, following the NaViT direction, so inputs can have variable resolutions and aspect ratios. The key observation is that high-resolution document pretraining is not always necessary if the model sees suitable spatial proxies: variable natural-image resolutions and variable rendered font sizes. Second, the model uses layout-aware visual augmentation. Text is rendered on the fly with diverse fonts, backgrounds, spatial layouts, and perturbations, reducing the chance that the encoder solves the task through font or canvas artifacts. Third, training combines natural image-text pairs and rendered text-text pairs under a single contrastive objective. Natural images are not merely auxiliary data; they provide grounding and prevent a text-only collapse in the visual domain. Fourth, multilingual learning uses a two-stage curriculum: large-scale unsupervised multilingual visual-text pretraining followed by semantic mid-training for cross-lingual alignment. This differs from prior fixed-resolution, rendered-text-only, single-stage recipes by treating resolution, layout, grounding, and multilinguality as coupled pretraining variables.55:T4a2,The experimental evidence is organized around four research questions. In the 13M-example ablation setting, variable natural-image resolutions and variable rendered font sizes improve transfer to resolution-sensitive document retrieval, supporting the spatial-proxy hypothesis. Removing natural image-text pairs severely degrades dense document retrieval, showing that even text-centric visual tasks benefit from real-world multimodal grounding. Fixed fonts and plain canvases lead to pixel-level shortcuts and near-collapse on document retrieval. For multilingual text, the best setup is a two-stage pipeline: unsupervised multilingual visual-text pretraining followed by semantic mid-training. At scale, Pixel Linguist II is trained on 280M examples and is evaluated on English, cross-lingual, and multilingual Visual STS, ViDoRe visual document retrieval, and as a vision encoder inside multimodal LLMs. The model also remains robust when up to 80% of visual tokens are compressed, which matters for document retrieval and serving cost. The provided text does not include the full numeric tables, so the most reliable signal is the structure of the ablations and their failure cases.56:T816,The method has two stages. First is AHAF, Adversarial Hallucination Attribute Flipping. Instead of using adversarial perturbations for standard classification attacks, the paper uses PGD to flip the model’s object-existence judgment on the same image, for example from present to absent or vice versa. This produces paired images that are semantically almost identical, with differences concentrated around the visual cues that move the model across a hallucination boundary. That is the key distinction from ordinary contrastive fine-tuning with random negatives: random negatives change too many factors at once, such as scene, object identity, and background, so the model can still solve the task through coarse spatial shortcuts.

Second is ACFT, Adversarial Contrastive Fine-Tuning. The model is fine-tuned with a contrastive objective built on AHAF-generated pairs, pulling correct image-text pairs together and pushing apart representations associated with hallucination-inducing perturbations. The important design choice is not merely the use of contrastive loss, but the controlled negative construction. Positive and negative samples share nearly all image content; the only systematic difference is the perturbation that flips the hallucination attribute. That makes the learning signal much more focused on the visual decision boundary.

AHAF also serves as a probe. If tiny pixel-level perturbations can flip the answer, then the visual representation is already sitting close to a fragile decision boundary even on clean inputs. That observation is more general than the specific method: many hallucination mitigation techniques may improve outputs without actually moving the underlying visual representation into a more stable region.

Compared with prior methods, VCD, OPERA, and VTI are more inference-time or post-hoc control methods; RLHF and DPO variants mainly reshape output preference; ordinary OCFT lacks targeted negatives. ACFT differs by directly training against the diagnosed visual misalignment mechanism, with no extra inference cost.57:T7ee,The most informative evidence comes from three groups of experiments.

First, diagnostic evidence. On LLaVA v1.5, hallucinated samples show much worse image-text embedding alignment than correct samples: average cosine similarity is 0.158 for correct cases versus -0.122 for hallucinated ones. Grad-CAM analysis shows an inverted attention pattern. When the target object is present, hallucinated cases have more dispersed attention; when the object is absent, they show overly concentrated attention on irrelevant regions. Using normalized Shannon entropy over 500 hallucinated and 500 non-hallucinated samples, entropy is 5.1% higher for hallucinated cases when the object is present, and 6.2% lower when the object is absent. This supports a visual-perception failure rather than pure language-side fabrication.

Second, causal intervention. The paper perturbs the visual side directly. On POPE, adding Gaussian noise, downsampling the image, or replacing the visual encoder with a weaker one reduces average accuracy from 0.842 to the 0.739-0.822 range. Replacing it with a stronger SigLIP-SO400M encoder raises accuracy to 0.864. This is an important step beyond correlation: changing visual feature quality systematically changes hallucination behavior.

Third, mitigation results. ACFT uses only 0.9% of COCO for fine-tuning and outperforms VCD, OPERA, VTI, and post-training approaches such as LLaVA-RLHF and DPO variants on POPE and MME, while preserving general visual understanding. The full tables are not included in the provided excerpt, but the paper repeatedly emphasizes three properties: high data efficiency, zero inference overhead, and a clear gain over ordinary contrastive fine-tuning because the negatives are controlled rather than random.

For a careful read, the most useful experimental sections are the OCFT versus ACFT comparison, the target-versus-non-target similarity-gap analysis, and the encoder-strength ablations. Those explain why the method works, not just that it improves benchmark scores.58:T509,Read it as a diagnosis-to-training paper. Start with the two findings and the causal validation in the introduction. The key question is whether the authors really close the loop from observation to intervention. Then go to the AHAF construction in the methods: how the PGD objective is defined, how the perturbation is constrained so it flips the hallucination attribute rather than destroying semantics, and how the paired samples enter the contrastive loss. That is where the paper either becomes a targeted hallucination method or collapses into generic adversarial robustness training.

In the experiments, prioritize four parts: the image-text similarity and Grad-CAM entropy analysis, the visual-side intervention studies, ACFT versus OCFT and prior mitigation methods, and the retention of general visual comprehension. If time is limited, Figures 1 to 3 and the main result tables are enough to capture the main argument in about 25 to 35 minutes.

Two deeper questions are worth keeping in mind. First, does AHAF mainly improve local robustness around adversarial boundaries, or does it genuinely improve broader visual alignment? Second, will the same mechanism hold for natively pretrained multimodal models, or is it especially suited to connector-style systems such as LLaVA?59:T524,The method is Adaptive Multi-Reward Projection, or AMRP. Instead of using a fixed weighted sum of reward dimensions throughout training, AMRP updates the projection weights online based on training dynamics. It uses three signals.

The first is relative shortfall, which measures whether a reward dimension is lagging behind the others. If a dimension remains under-optimized, its weight is increased. The second is reward volatility, which tracks instability in a dimension. High volatility suggests that the policy has not stabilized there, so AMRP keeps attention on it. The third is recent progress, which measures whether a dimension is improving, stagnating, or regressing. Saturated and stable dimensions get down-weighted; stagnant or regressing dimensions get up-weighted.

This design addresses the two identified failure modes in a practical way. It does not eliminate the information loss of scalarization entirely, but it reduces persistent aliasing by making the projection time-varying rather than static. More importantly, it counters shortcut lock-in by reallocating learning pressure toward neglected dimensions. Compared with manual reward tuning or simple normalization, the key difference is that AMRP is driven by observed reward dynamics during training rather than by fixed prior assumptions.5a:T4a0,The evaluation covers three multi-reward settings: rule-based mathematical reasoning, citation-grounded generation with automatic evaluators, and open-ended alignment using learned reward-model scores. The main empirical claim is that AMRP consistently improves downstream performance across all three, with especially strong gains in math reasoning and citation-grounded generation, and smaller but still consistent gains in the more correlated open-ended alignment setting.

The paper also provides mechanism-level evidence. It shows that under static aggregation, training is often dominated early by dense rewards such as format or length, while harder dimensions such as correctness remain under-optimized. AMRP changes the reward-profile dynamics by shifting weight toward lagging or unstable dimensions, which leads to more balanced profiles over time. Another useful result is compatibility: the method composes with PPO, GRPO, and GDPO, suggesting that it is best viewed as a reward-aggregation module rather than a replacement for the RL optimizer itself. The emphasis is less on one dramatic benchmark number and more on consistent gains plus healthier reward trajectories.5b:T793,The method, TUSA, inserts a training-free cognitive arbiter between the base model policy πθ and the weak specialist πϕ. Instead of combining the two distributions at every step, the system first lets the base model propose a candidate token and then decides whether the specialist is allowed to intervene. The decision is made by a gate defined through a Joint Necessity Score.

That score combines two signals. The first is supervisor confidence, operationalized mainly through token-level entropy of the specialist distribution. Low entropy means the specialist has a sharper belief over the next token and is more likely to provide meaningful guidance. The second is semantic saliency. The paper argues that many decoding positions carry little semantic load, so spending intervention budget on function words or low-information tokens is wasteful even if the specialist is confident there.

The arbiter activates only when both conditions hold: the specialist is confident enough, and the current token is semantically important enough. This is stronger than a plain entropy threshold. Confidence alone would still trigger many low-value interventions; saliency alone would allow the specialist to overwrite the base model even when it is unsure. Their conjunction turns alignment into trust-based arbitration rather than continuous supervision.

Conceptually, the contribution is less about a new learned reward model and more about a new control logic at decoding time. Compared with dense guidance methods such as MARA-style token-level steering, TUSA changes three things: intervention becomes event-triggered rather than always-on; authorization depends on uncertainty-aware gating rather than fixed fusion; and the objective shifts from maximizing supervision coverage to minimizing harmful low-confidence interference. In that sense, the method is closer to abstention and selective prediction than to stronger supervision.5c:T630,The experiments address two questions: whether weak specialists are indeed persistently uncertain, and whether sparse intervention outperforms dense intervention. Evaluations are conducted on Mistral- and Llama-family backbones over both general preference benchmarks and safety benchmarks.

First, the paper validates the phenomenon directly. Figure 2 shows entropy profiles of the specialist across domains. Entropy is frequently close to the theoretical maximum for a large fraction of tokens, and this high uncertainty often persists along the generation trajectory. This supports the claim that the confidence–intervention mismatch is structural rather than anecdotal.

Second, on end metrics, TUSA reduces intervention overhead by roughly 50% relative to the dense baseline. At the same time, it improves Preference Rate by up to 12.0% on general benchmarks and by up to 15.6% on safety benchmarks. The key point is that gains come from fewer but better interventions, not from stronger or more frequent overwriting.

Third, the analysis suggests that sparse gating better preserves valid reasoning paths from the base model. Dense methods are more likely to derail correct trajectories when the specialist is uncertain. Ablations indicate that removing either uncertainty or semantic saliency weakens performance, implying the two signals are complementary. The paper is especially useful for its utility–safety trade-off analysis: the method does not simply increase refusal behavior, but improves alignment while preserving more of the base model’s original competence.5d:T42b,Read it with one question in mind: when should a decoding-time controller abstain? Start with the introduction and Figures 1–2. They contain the main empirical claim and explain why the paper is not merely proposing another gating heuristic. Then focus on the method section where the dense aligned policy is reformulated into a conditional intervention policy. The most important part is how the Joint Necessity Score is built and why both uncertainty and semantic saliency are required.

In the experiments, prioritize three pieces. First, the entropy distribution plots and trajectory-level uncertainty analysis. Second, the trade-off between intervention rate and preference/safety outcomes. Third, the ablations comparing full TUSA against entropy-only, saliency-only, and threshold variants. A quick read takes about 30 minutes. A deeper read should examine whether entropy is a sufficient proxy for local supervisory competence, and whether the same arbitration logic could be extended to verifier-guided decoding, reward models, or multi-supervisor systems.5e:T671,OSW-FT keeps the standard expert trajectory as the optimization target, but rescales its loss online. For each query q, the current model runs K inference-only rollouts to estimate a pass rate p-hat(q). The usual SFT loss on the expert output o* is then multiplied by (1 - p-hat(q)). Queries the model already solves reliably get downweighted; unresolved frontier queries get larger updates.

The design choice is important. The method does not optimize on sampled trajectories, does not apply token-level reward shaping, and does not alter the training distribution. The rollout is used only to estimate a scalar weight. This sharply separates it from PPO/GRPO-style methods, where the sampled trajectories both define the learning signal and receive the update.

The theoretical framing is built for binary-verifiable reasoning. The authors start from expected correctness, apply the score-function trick, and introduce a query-dependent control variate. This yields a variance-reduced estimator whose optimal baseline depends on the model’s success probability on that query. That connection motivates why scaling the expert log-likelihood gradient by a function of success rate is sensible. They further argue that with finite K, the Monte Carlo estimate remains unbiased for the exact OSW-FT surrogate update, so rollout noise affects variance but not the target update itself.

Conceptually, this is best read as online difficulty-aware SFT rather than offline RL or reward-weighted regression. The online component estimates whether an example is still informative; the offline expert trajectory still specifies what the model should learn.5f:T5a9,The experiments use Qwen3 models from 0.6B to 4B parameters on binary-verifiable reasoning benchmarks including AIME, AMC, MATH-500, and GPQA-Diamond. The empirical claim is deliberately modest: OSW-FT consistently improves over standard SFT at small-to-medium scales, but it is not presented as a universal replacement for GRPO.

The results support four main points. First, adaptive online weighting beats uniform SFT across multiple model sizes, which suggests that competence-aware reweighting is useful even when the supervision target stays fixed. Second, under limited rollout budgets, OSW-FT often offers a better compute-performance trade-off because it only needs a few inference-only samples per query and avoids the full RL optimization loop. Third, the comparison with GRPO is mixed in the expected way: OSW-FT is favorable in several low-budget settings, while GRPO remains stronger in some configurations. Fourth, the ablations matter: continuous success-rate weighting outperforms hard-thresholding and random weighting, indicating that the gain comes from a smooth online estimate of current competence rather than generic sample reweighting.

The provided full text excerpt does not include all table values, so exact benchmark deltas are not fully recoverable here. Still, the paper is clear about the pattern it wants to establish: consistent gains over SFT across 0.6B–4B models, especially when rollout budget is constrained.60:T486,The paper connects MH acceptance ratios to Bradley–Terry (BT) choice models. Under BT, the odds of preferring x over y equal exp(u(x)-u(y)), which matches the unnormalized density ratio needed by MH for a Gibbs target. The obstacle is that the algorithm cannot query the comparison probability itself—only draw Bernoulli preference samples. The authors prove an impossibility result: any fixed-budget plug-in estimator of the acceptance ratio (estimate win probabilities then substitute into MH) cannot implement the oracle acceptance rule and can arbitrarily distort the stationary distribution. Pref-MH avoids estimating probabilities and instead designs a randomized accept/reject mechanism whose marginal acceptance probability exactly matches the Hastings ratio implied by the preference odds (plus proposal correction). With this construction, detailed balance holds in expectation, yielding an exact stationary distribution while using only a fixed number of binary comparisons per transition. The method is stated for general state spaces and arbitrary proposal kernels, positioning it as a general-purpose MCMC wrapper around preference oracles.61:T4b6,The proposed mechanism is trait-direction drift:
- Define a teacher-side preference gap for each sample (p,r) as the log-likelihood difference between a biased system prompt and a set of neutral prompts, with a length-normalized variant to compare variable-length completions.
- Use an empirically motivated low-rank/logit-linear factorization of the prompt-by-sample log-probability matrix (via SVD-like geometry): prompt embeddings ψ(s) and sample embeddings φ(p,r) explain why certain “innocent” samples still carry trait signal.
- Argue (and test) that student SFT updates contain a component aligned with a recognizable trait direction; across steps this component accumulates beyond noise, producing measurable drift and eventually behavioral transfer (preference shifts or higher misalignment rates).
- Introduce probe-space corridor regularization: monitor the student’s coordinate along a probed trait/steering subspace and penalize deviations outside a corridor, limiting motion along the trait direction while keeping general task learning largely intact.
The paper also studies why cross-model subliminal transfer is weaker than same-model transfer, using diagnostics and interventions.62:T897,方法叫 FactAgent，核心由两部分组成：fact-centric agent state，以及基于 Bayesian fact utility estimation 的 dense process supervision。

先看 agent 形式。与标准 ReAct 只维护原始对话和工具返回文本不同，FactAgent 在状态里显式维护一个 fact store。每次 search 动作得到 observation 后，agent 不只是继续推理，还可以执行 assert 动作，把 observation 里的信息抽取成结构化事实，写入 fact store。这样，状态从“越来越长的文本历史”变成“问题 Q、当前 observation、累计事实集合 Ft”。作者把这些事实视为离散语义单元，而不是普通字符串，这一步是后续 credit assignment 的基础。

再看奖励构造。论文的关键不是直接给 assert 打启发式分，而是从 group rollouts 里估计事实的后验效用。具体做法是先把语义等价的事实聚成 cluster，避免同一证据因表述不同被分散统计；然后基于一组 rollout 的最终 outcome，用 Bayesian estimation 推断每个 fact cluster 与成功结果的统计关联。直观上，如果某类事实经常出现在成功轨迹里、较少出现在失败轨迹里，它的 posterior utility 就更高。随后，这个 fact-level utility 被映射回 step-level dense rewards，用来训练 policy。

这比直接做 token-level process reward 更适合搜索任务，因为搜索任务的有效中间单位不是 token，也不一定是整句 reasoning，而是“找到并确认了哪条证据”。作者因此把 credit assignment 的对象从 action surface form 转成 evidence semantics。和纯 outcome-based GRPO 相比，FactAgent 仍然利用 group rollouts，但不再让整条轨迹共享同一个粗粒度 advantage，而是通过事实聚类和后验估计，把奖励分配到真正有贡献的中间步骤上。

从设计取舍看，这个方法避免了额外训练 reward model，也不依赖人工标注每一步是否正确，代价则是需要可靠的事实抽取与聚类。如果抽取质量差，后验 utility 估计会被噪声污染。全文也承认这一点，因此 fact store 既是方法优势，也是误差传播的主要入口。63:T89d,FactAgent has two main components: a fact-centric state representation and a Bayesian utility estimation scheme for dense process supervision.

On the agent side, the paper departs from standard ReAct-style agents that keep only raw text history. The state explicitly includes a fact store. After a search action returns an observation, the agent can take an assert action that extracts structured facts from the observation and writes them into the store. The state is therefore represented as the question, the current observation, and the accumulated fact set. These facts are treated as discrete semantic evidence units rather than plain text snippets. This is important because the method assigns credit at the level of evidence semantics, not at the level of surface-form actions.

On the reward side, the paper estimates fact utility from grouped rollouts. Semantically equivalent facts are first clustered so that the same evidence is not split across multiple textual variants. Then, using final outcomes from a group of trajectories, the method performs Bayesian estimation of the posterior utility of each fact cluster. Intuitively, if a fact cluster appears disproportionately often in successful trajectories, its posterior utility increases. These fact-level utilities are then converted into dense step-level rewards for policy optimization.

This differs from standard outcome-based GRPO, where all steps in a trajectory inherit the same coarse trajectory-level signal. Here, group rollouts are still used, but the reward is redistributed through an explicit evidence layer. That makes the method better matched to search tasks, where the meaningful intermediate unit is often not a token or a reasoning sentence, but a retrieved and validated piece of evidence.

The design also avoids training an external reward model and does not require human annotation of intermediate steps. The trade-off is that the whole pipeline depends on the quality of fact extraction and fact clustering. If those are noisy, the posterior utility estimates can be corrupted. The paper is aware of this: the fact store is both the main strength of the method and the main source of possible error propagation.64:T5ef,The experiments use Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct backbones and evaluate on seven QA benchmarks. The headline result is that with the 7B backbone, FactAgent reaches an average EM of 51.2, outperforming the strongest baseline by +3.2 EM. The paper emphasizes that the gains are consistent across benchmarks rather than driven by a single dataset.

The more informative findings come from the analysis. The dense process supervision mainly improves evidence precision and retrieval quality. That matches the method design: the reward does not simply encourage more search, but encourages finding useful evidence and committing it into the explicit state.

The ablations likely cover the main components: removing the fact store, removing the assert action, disabling fact clustering, or replacing Bayesian posterior utility with simpler statistics. From the full-text description, the explicit fact representation and the utility estimation module are the most important contributors. Another practical point is that the method derives dense rewards directly from grouped rollout outcomes, without relying on an external reward model, which makes the training setup lighter and easier to scale.

The current evidence mainly supports search-heavy QA agents. Whether the same approach transfers to broader web agents, coding agents, or longer-horizon multi-tool settings depends on whether “facts” remain the right intermediate abstraction and whether fact extraction stays reliable in those environments.65:T427,Start with the introduction and preliminaries to see that the paper changes more than the reward function. It jointly changes the state representation, the action space, and the reward decomposition. Then focus on three technical pieces: how the fact store is defined, how the assert action extracts and writes facts, and how Bayesian utility estimation is computed from grouped rollouts. The key questions while reading are: what exactly counts as a fact, how semantic equivalence is handled, and how posterior fact utility is mapped back to step-level rewards.

In the experiments, prioritize the main results table, the analyses on evidence precision and retrieval quality, and the ablations on fact store and utility estimation. A skim in about 40 minutes is enough to capture the main idea. A deeper read should ask whether explicit intermediate state could replace part of free-form chain-of-thought in reasoning RL, and whether fact-level credit assignment could generalize to code execution states, web interaction states, or multimodal tool trajectories.66:T53e,The method has two main components. The evaluation pipeline samples from roughly 100k monthly production queries while balancing diversity and representativeness. Uniform sampling preserves the production distribution but keeps many near-duplicate templates. Diversity-first sampling increases coverage but distorts the service and task mix. The paper introduces a template-aware sampler: mask variable spans, cluster near-identical normalized prompts with LSH, select diverse examples within each template using greedy max-min over variable spans, and allocate budget proportional to the square root of template count. Scoring is task-specific. Classification and information extraction, about 63.2% of traffic, use reference-based evaluation with gold answers generated by Kimi-K2.5 and checked by annotators. Open-ended summarization and generation use side-by-side judging augmented with checklist-style rubrics. The training recipe starts from Qwen3-32B with a Cyrillic-dense tokenizer and runs in non-reasoning mode for latency and cost reasons. It performs a combined SFT stage, then trains separate GRPO experts for instruction following, function calling, and internal task-distribution alignment. The experts are merged with two-stage SLERP in weight space to avoid cross-domain reward interference from a single joint RL objective.67:T4a5,The deployed adapted model absorbed 50% of platform traffic six months after rollout, corresponding to 116 million requests per month from more than 200 internal applications. On benchmark construction, the template-aware sampler reaches a mean pairwise TF-IDF cosine distance of 0.953 while keeping Jensen-Shannon distances lower than pure diversity sampling, indicating better coverage without strongly drifting from production mix. For judging, a uniform side-by-side LLM judge has limited agreement with experts, with Cohen’s kappa around 0.62 and task-dependent errors. After task routing, the LLM task classifier matches human consensus 90.6% to 99.6% of the time. The final task-specific judging pipeline improves agreement: reference-based subtasks rise from kappa 0.63 to 0.88, and open-ended content-generation subtasks rise from 0.57 to 0.72. For content generation, explicit per-criterion grading plus an overall verdict reaches kappa 0.79. The authors also release an open-weight checkpoint trained without internal data and report that it is close to the deployed model on public benchmarks, suggesting that the recipe contributes substantially beyond private traffic data.68:T43e,1) Controlled conflict setup: a fact-retrieval prompt template that mimics multimodal RAG—textual supplied context (containing a plausible but counterfactual answer) + entity (either name text or an image) + query. Only the entity modality changes.
2) Dataset: a curated benchmark of 37K conflicting fact pairs across celebrities, buildings, and artworks.
3) Validity filtering: they keep only instances where the model can (a) identify the entity and (b) recall the parametric fact when no conflict is present, so the measured effect is conflict resolution rather than recognition failure.
4) Mechanistic claim: for textual entities, attention detects the conflict and suppresses early-layer MLPs associated with factual recall (building on the “MLPs store facts” line of work). For visual entities, entity resolution is slower, so the suppression does not happen in time, leading to more parametric answers.
5) Prompt interventions: CoT prompting does not close the modality gap; adding more image-grounded contextual information reduces the parametric bias for visual entities.69:T49c,They operate on Cross-layer Transcoders (CLTs), which learn a shared sparse dictionary spanning all layers (a cross-depth latent space), enabling analysis beyond single-layer SAEs.
They compare three latent-selection methods:
- ValSel: select latents with language-distinct activation magnitudes (mean activation differences), using an l-monolinguality score.
- FreqSel: select latents that activate more frequently for a target language, with a specificity threshold T and corpus/token coverage constraints (N%, M%).
- AnnSel: trace attribution graphs with Circuit Tracer, prune to high-importance paths, then filter latents by LLM-generated annotations that explicitly mention the target language (favoring interpretability).
Given per-language latent sets, they test four intervention families at inference:
- Target amplification (set target latents to mean nonzero activations from target-language data).
- Distractor zero ablation (zero latents for the context language or all non-target languages).
- Distractor direction ablation (remove residual projections along distractor latent directions; one-layer vs multi-layer).
- Combined interventions (e.g., Zero+Amp, 1L+Amp).6a:T6bc,The main technical contribution is an analysis-ready perturbation framework plus a mechanistic probing pipeline. Existing adversarial summarization datasets are usually too coarse for causal tracing because they modify text at the sentence or document level. The authors instead build paired clean/corrupt summaries with explicit token-level modification maps. They define eight localized perturbation types spanning two quality axes, Readability and Adequacy, while controlling perturbation intensity through a parameter k and preserving exact token positions of edits.

Using this setup, they analyze two judge models: Themis, built on Llama-3-8B, and Prometheus, built on Mistral-7B. The experiments combine causal tracing, logit-lens vocabulary projection, and attention-head knockout. The central finding is a two-stage internal evaluation process. Lower layers, especially attention modules, are mainly involved in defect identification and signal routing. Upper-layer MLPs integrate those signals into a final rating, with the score becoming sharply encoded in the residual stream at a late layer. The paper also shows criterion-specific attention behavior: readability errors spread across nearby syntactic context, while adequacy-related perturbations stay more localized around the modified tokens.

Another useful result is the comparison with the base model. Fine-tuning does not appear to create the evaluation pipeline from scratch. The base Llama-3-8B already exhibits a routing-like substrate and late-layer score crystallization. Judge fine-tuning mainly modifies that substrate by suppressing low-layer MLP contribution at the last position and moving the crystallization point earlier by about two layers.6b:T4b2,The most important results are mechanistic. Both judges show a consistent stage split: below roughly layer 15, attention dominates error detection and routing; around layers 25 to 26, the final rating becomes sharply represented. Themis writes the score in a more concentrated top-layer step, while Prometheus distributes the write across mid-to-late layers and applies an opposite-direction correction at the final layer. The comparison to base Llama-3-8B is also notable: the base model already contains much of the routing structure, and judge fine-tuning mainly reshapes information flow rather than inventing a new circuit.

The perturbation analysis further shows that different error types induce different attention patterns. Readability defects recruit broader local context, whereas adequacy defects remain more tightly centered on the perturbed tokens. This suggests that criterion-specific supervision could be a sensible design direction for future evaluator training.

If reading selectively, the key is not every individual attack category but the connection between token-level perturbation maps, score shifts, and layer-wise decomposition of attention, MLP, and residual contributions.6c:T899,论文的技术路线分三步。

第一步是现象隔离。作者比较 released VLM 与对应 reference LLM 的文本能力差距，并引入 text-only further-training 对照，以及共享 base LLM 的 VL-versus-text matched trajectory，说明损失不是一般继续训练都会发生，而是和视觉模态适配强相关。损失最明显的不是开放式知识问答，而是那些对表面 token 格式、答案边界、最终输出规则很敏感的任务。

第二步是机制诊断。作者把问题归因到 attention-sink corruption。现代 LLM 往往会把大量注意力质量集中到少数早期位置，这些位置的 hidden state 又被少数高幅值固定特征维度主导，形成 sink。论文的假设是，这种 sink 有助于稳定每个位置对关键表面 token 的注意力分配，从而支撑格式敏感任务。VL fine-tuning 会扰动 query/key 投影 Wq、Wk；由于 logit shift 会随输入幅值放大，这种扰动在 sink 位置被特别放大，导致 per-head sink collapse。作者进一步分析不同 QK normalization 设计的差异：如果是 per-head QK-RMSNorm，可以直接去掉对原始输入幅值的显式依赖；如果只是 layerwise normalization，则每个 head 的尺度仍受整层能量分配影响，弱 head 会被进一步压缩，因此到 VL 训练开始前，某些 backbone 的 per-head sink 已经偏弱。

第三步是预测指标与干预验证。作者提出 Sink Strength，一个只在 base LLM 上通过少量 forward pass 计算的标量，用来量化 head-level sink concentration。这个指标不需要做任何 VL 训练，就能预测后续多模态适配后的文本能力损失。然后他们把 base-side sink weakness 与 post-VL gap perturbation 结合起来，区分“骨干先天脆弱”和“VL 更新额外破坏”两部分。最后做两个负对照：一是在预训练后给模型补装 QK-RMSNorm 再去做 VL 训练，发现不能复现原生架构自带的保护效果；二是对已经训练好的 VLM 做多种 weight merging，也无法把丢失的文本能力救回来。这说明问题更像训练过程中的 head-level 结构破坏，而不是事后线性修补能解决的。6d:T843,The paper proceeds in three stages.

First, it isolates the phenomenon. The authors compare released VLMs against their reference LLM backbones on a text-only evaluation suite, and add text-only further-training controls plus a matched VL-versus-text training trajectory from the same base model. This is meant to show that the degradation is not just a generic consequence of continued training, but is tied to multimodal adaptation.

Second, it proposes a mechanism: attention-sink corruption. Many modern LLMs place a large fraction of attention mass on a few early positions. Those positions are dominated by a small set of high-magnitude feature dimensions and act as sinks. The paper argues that these sinks help stabilize attention allocation in ways that matter for tasks graded on exact output form. During VL fine-tuning, perturbations to the query and key projections, Wq and Wk, induce logit shifts that scale with input magnitude. Because sink positions have unusually large magnitude, the perturbation is amplified there, causing per-head sink collapse. The architecture analysis then distinguishes per-head QK-RMSNorm from layerwise normalization: per-head normalization removes explicit raw-magnitude dependence, while layerwise normalization can still compress weak heads because each head’s scale is tied to the layer’s total energy.

Third, the paper introduces a predictive metric and tests interventions. Sink Strength is a single scalar computed on the base LLM, before VL training, to quantify head-level sink concentration. It predicts how much text capability will be lost after VL adaptation. The authors then separate base-side vulnerability from update-induced corruption by combining pre-VL sink weakness with post-VL sink-gap perturbation. Finally, they run two negative controls: injecting QK-RMSNorm after pretraining and then doing VL training, and applying several post-VL weight-merging methods. Neither recovers the protection seen in native architectures, which suggests the issue is tied to training-time head-level dynamics rather than something easily repaired afterward.6e:T64f,The study covers six released VLM–LLM pairs from families including Qwen, InternVL, and LLaVA-OneVision, with headline examples such as Qwen3-VL, InternVL3.5, Qwen2.5-VL, InternVL3, and LLaVA-OV, plus additional controls. The language backbone is extracted from each VLM and evaluated as a standalone causal LM on a nine-task text suite against a reference LLM.

Several results stand out. First, the text-capability gap reaches double-digit points on multiple format-sensitive tasks, while matched text-only further training causes much smaller degradation. That supports a modality-specific effect. Second, Sink Strength is a strong predictor of post-VL damage: the headline rank correlation is Spearman rho = 0.97, and correlations remain high across six pairs and four format-sensitive tasks, with rho >= 0.88. Third, the preserved-sink versus collapsed-sink split is sharp on instruction following: every preserved-sink backbone stays within 5.4 points of its reference LLM, while every collapsed-sink backbone loses at least 7.9 points. Fourth, native per-head QK-RMSNorm is associated with better sink preservation, but post-pretraining injection of the same module does not reproduce the benefit. Fifth, post-VL weight merging, including linear, spectral, and sparsified variants, fails to recover the lost text capability under the tested settings.

The strongest part of the empirical design is the control structure. The paper does not rely on a single correlation plot; it includes text-only controls, matched training trajectories, architecture comparisons, and negative controls for post-hoc repair.6f:T4d0,Read it in the order of phenomenon, mechanism, prediction, and failed repair. Start with the setup and the definition of format-sensitive tasks, then move to the Sink Strength section. The important point is that Sink Strength is computed entirely on the base LLM, before any VL training, so it is a pre-adaptation diagnostic rather than a post-hoc explanation.

Then spend time on the mechanism section. The most informative part of the paper is the chain from sink positions to Wq/Wk perturbation amplification to degraded format-sensitive behavior. Also pay attention to the distinction between per-head and layerwise QK normalization. That architectural detail is central to the argument.

After that, inspect the ranking results and the preserved-sink vs collapsed-sink grouping, then read the two negative controls carefully. They are what make the paper more convincing than a simple predictor paper: adding QK-RMSNorm after pretraining does not recreate the benefit, and merging weights after VL training does not restore the lost capability.

A selective read takes about 40 minutes. If you want to understand Theorem 1, the margin calibration, and the normalization-granularity argument in detail, budget 70 to 90 minutes.70:T780,AnySearch formulates budget-aware search as a resource-constrained sequential decision problem. The state contains the interaction history h_t and remaining budget b_t. The action space has three actions: reason internally, call the search tool, or answer and terminate. Budget is charged only on external search calls, since search latency and retrieved-document tokens dominate cost relative to internal reasoning tokens.

The method uses a two-phase curriculum. Phase I is a scaffolded learning stage. The model receives explicit budget-state injection and structured reasoning prompts that encourage it to think about whether a search is necessary, how much budget remains, and how to allocate it. Budgets decay linearly during training so the policy sees a range of budget regimes instead of overfitting to a single point.

Phase II removes the scaffold entirely. Training inputs now match inference conditions: the model is given only the total budget and must autonomously manage allocation. To make this work across budgets, the paper introduces adaptive budget sampling. Rather than sampling budgets uniformly, it focuses more on budget levels where the current policy is weak, while retaining uniform smoothing for coverage. This is important because the target is not peak performance at one budget, but a stable performance curve over many budgets.

The reward is a composite objective combining answer accuracy and budget efficiency. Efficiency is measured with both absolute and relative signals. A key detail is the adaptive weighting of the efficiency term based on query difficulty, which prevents the policy from becoming overly conservative on hard questions. Without this, a strong efficiency reward would push the model toward under-searching; too weak a reward would collapse back to wasteful search. The paper’s solution is effectively a difficulty-aware trade-off controller inside RL training.71:T660,The evaluation spans 7 benchmarks and 3 backbone models. The main result is consistent improvement over baselines across all tested budget levels, together with generalization to budget values not seen during training. That unseen-budget result is more important than a single average score, because it suggests the model learned a transferable budget-allocation behavior rather than memorizing a few discrete budget settings.

The baselines include fixed-budget RL search agents, reward-shaping methods that try to reduce redundant tool use without an explicit budget interface, and BATS-style approaches that rely on an external budget tracker. AnySearch improves not only answer quality but also tool productivity, meaning each search call yields more useful information on average. It also achieves the lowest total token consumption, which indicates the gain is not coming from replacing search with excessive internal reasoning tokens.

The ablations address three central questions. First, whether the scaffold is truly internalized: after removing explicit budget tracking and structured prompts, performance remains strong, which supports the internalization claim. Second, whether the two-phase curriculum is necessary: removing either the scaffolded phase or the scaffold-free adaptation phase hurts cross-budget robustness. Third, whether each reward component matters: removing relative efficiency, absolute efficiency, or adaptive weighting degrades the accuracy–efficiency trade-off. The paper emphasizes consistency across the full budget range and generalization to unseen budgets more than isolated peak numbers.72:T515,A three-stage simulation pipeline:
1) Data-driven persona building: start from anonymized, aggregated behavioral signals (activity patterns, engagement, inferred demographics) and use an LLM to synthesize a structured persona profile (summaries, patterns, narrative descriptors) used as conditioning context.
2) Structured elicitation: present control/treatment variants (visual stimuli) plus a target metric (CTR/subscription) via carefully designed structured questions; each persona-conditioned agent outputs numeric scores per variant and persona-grounded rationales. Prompt format is treated as a key hyperparameter.
3) Statistical aggregation: aggregate per-persona score differences into a simulated treatment-effect distribution \(\mathcal{N}(\hat{\delta}_s, \hat{\sigma}_s)\), analogous to the normal approximation of the real experiment’s relative effect \(\mathcal{N}(\hat{\delta}_{rel}, \hat{\sigma}^2_{rel})\). Quality is mainly measured by sign agreement (improve/harm/no effect). The paper further studies persona-pool subsampling to reduce inference cost.
The main methodological distinction from prior systems is grounding personas in real behavioral signals (vs screenshot-derived synthetic personas) and systematically analyzing prompt format + persona depth/diversity + sampling.73:T467,AgentFactory formulates an agentic system \(s\) as a pair drawn from a joint space \(\mathcal{S} = \mathcal{M} \times \mathcal{W}\):
- Model space \(\mathcal{M}\): includes selecting a foundation model from an available set, plus optional instruction fine-tuning choices—dataset \(\mathcal{D}\), hyperparameters \(\mathcal{H}\), and tuning method \(\phi\) (LoRA/QLoRA/full FT). For closed models, fine-tuning is disabled (empty \(d,h\)).
- Workflow space \(\mathcal{W}\): can be graphs, neural workflow representations, or executable code.
- Multi-objective evaluation: a vector of metrics \(\mathbf{g}(s_i)\) computed by evaluation functions \(\mathbf{G} = \{G_1,\dots,G_p\}\) (accuracy, cost, latency, etc.).
- Optimization: because the space is non-differentiable and partially observable, the framework uses an “LLM optimizer” to propose iterative modifications based on the history (trajectory) of candidate systems and their metric vectors.
The key delta vs prior automated workflow optimizers is explicitly incorporating model fine-tuning into the automated search loop and emphasizing multi-objective constraints.74:T7e4,The method is an alternating optimization framework called WHALE. The joint objective is to maximize verifier reward over trajectories induced by both model parameters θ and harness h. Directly updating both at once creates a credit-assignment problem: harness search is a slow proposal-evaluate-accept loop, while online finetuning alternates rollout collection and gradient updates at a much faster cadence. WHALE avoids this mismatch by decomposing the problem into two conditional subproblems:
1) update weights with the harness fixed;
2) search for a better harness with the updated model fixed.

For the weight phase, the paper uses online RSFT. Under the current harness, the model samples multiple trajectories per prompt, keeps only verifier-accepted rollouts, and trains with supervised learning on those accepted traces. This choice is more stable than direct policy-gradient style updates and fits existing SFT pipelines well.

For the harness phase, the paper uses Meta-Harness to search over executable harness programs while keeping the model fixed. The search space includes system instructions, tool schemas, context management, parser/execution logic, and termination policy, which is much broader than prompt-only optimization.

The paper also introduces adaptive WHALE. A fixed schedule must decide how long each phase should run, but that is hard to tune: if a phase is too short, it lacks evidence; if too long, it over-optimizes against a frozen counterpart. Adaptive WHALE enforces a minimum phase length, then switches when the current phase’s training signal stops improving. This is a sensible design because the bottleneck can shift during training.

Compared with prior joint-adaptation work, the main differences are clear. Earlier methods typically co-optimize weights and textual prompts. WHALE expands the optimization target to full executable harnesses and uses conditional alternation rather than concurrent co-adaptation, prioritizing stable attribution over raw update frequency.75:T7b3,The evaluation covers three domains: SearchQA, mathematical reasoning with code execution, and chess puzzles. Baselines include weight-only optimization, harness-only optimization, and Fast-Slow Training (FST). Importantly, their FST implementation uses the same update methods and schedule as WHALE, but restricts the search space to system/user prompts. This isolates the effect of searching over a full harness.

Main results: WHALE improves over single-component baselines by 7.67 to 24.38 percentage points, and over FST by 4.15 to 13.00 points. That suggests the gain is not just from extra tuning, but from the alternating scheme plus the richer harness search space.

A particularly useful finding is the existence of different bottleneck regimes:
- In SearchQA, harness-only search can match the peak accuracy of weight-only training with far fewer rollouts. This indicates a harness-limited regime, where retrieval and context/control logic dominate.
- In Math, harness-only search barely helps until a small weight update is applied first. After that, the same harness search becomes effective. This is a weight-limited regime, where the model’s reasoning or code-use ability must cross a threshold before harness improvements matter.

This observation explains why stagewise optimization is weak: it assumes the bottleneck stays on one side for a long stretch, while the experiments show that the bottleneck can move.

The paper also compares alternating updates against stagewise optimization directly. Under matched budgets, small alternating updates outperform stagewise by 5.32 points on SearchQA and 9.16 points on Math, and surpass the final stagewise result after using only 29% and 49% as many rollouts, respectively. That is a strong sample-efficiency result.

Finally, adaptive WHALE beats the tuned fixed schedule on both evaluated domains, supporting the claim that phase duration should respond to the training signal rather than remain static.a:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 overflow-y-auto","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L14",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-09-03","generated_at":"2026-09-03T00:51:28.401028+00:00","stats":{"total":152,"starred":6,"must_read":11,"skim":92,"glance":49,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":3,"industry":3,"llm_pretrain":2,"scaling":5,"architecture":11,"efficient":20,"eval":24,"multimodal":15,"multimodal_gen":4,"post_train":20,"mechanism":25,"coding_agent":5,"agent":15},"trending_repos":10,"trending_hf":10,"fetch_status":{"arxiv":"ok","huggingface":"failed","blog":"ok","twitter":"empty","github_trending":"ok","hf_trending":"ok"},"fetch_errors":{"huggingface":"exhausted 5 retries; last error: Client error '400 Bad Request' for url 'https://huggingface.co/api/daily_papers?date=2026-09-03'\nFor more information check: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Status/400"},"last_fetch_attempt":1788398181.467022},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2609.00378","title":"Removable and Irreducible: A Token-Cost Ledger for the Multilingual Tokenization Tax","abstract":"Large language models pay a well-documented tax on non-English text: the same content costs several times more tokens, and because attention is quadratic in sequence length, far more compute. Through controlled experiments, we find that forward Kullback-Leibler (KL) distillation--the standard KD formulation--with post-trained teachers behaves fundamentally differently during mid-training, an intermediate phase of self-supervised learning on curated corpora. for","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2609_01532.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这项工作解决的是一个很具体但很关键的问题：logit-based KD 在 mid-training 阶段是否仍然像 pre-training 阶段一样稳定有效。作者发现答案是否定的。标准 forward KL KD 在 pre-training 中能同时提升 reasoning 和 factual recall，但在 mid-training 中会出现清晰的 reasoning-recall tradeoff：推理继续受益，事实记忆获取反而变慢。问题不在于 efficiency 是实际训练 recipe 里的核心问题。第二，它指出 post-trained teacher 并不总是给 mid-training student 提供均衡监督。教师在程序性、数学、指令类数据上更自信，在开放网页和未结构化事实上熵更高；而 mid-training 时尚未学到的事实，往往正落在高熵区域。第三，Switch Distillation 给出一个低成本修正：用教师预测熵决定 token 走 KD 还是 NTP。这类方法对 mid-train、cooldown、continual pretrain 都有直接参考价值，因为它把蒸馏强度从全局超参变成了按 token 自适应的训练信号选择。","technical_approach":"基础目标是标准 next-token prediction 与 KL distillation 的插值，α 控制蒸馏强度，教师和学生使用温度缩放后的预测分布。作者比较了 forward KL 和 reverse KL，也扫描了教师规模和插值系数。关键发现不是某个 KL 方向优劣，而是 mid-training 下没有一个固定 KD 目标能在 reasoning 和 recall 上同时支配 NTP。机制分析围绕教师预测熵展开。低熵 token 通常对应教师更确定的程序性监督，例如数学步骤、指令格式、代码或结构化模式；高熵 token 更多对应开放事实、网页文本和存在多种合理续写的场景。KD 在高熵处会稀释 one-hot ground-truth 的学习信号，学生不再像 NTP 那样强烈追逐真实下一个 token。pre-training 早期学生知识少，KD 的软分布整体有帮助；但到 mid-training 起点，学生已经在 生态做受控对比，核心学生规模为 1B，并使用 7B、13B 级别教师，包含 instruct teacher。作者分别考察 pre-training 与 mid-training。结果显示，在 pre-training 中，forward KL KD 相比 NTP 同时改善 reasoning 和 factual recall；但在 mid-training 中，增加 α 通常提升 reasoning，同时压低或减慢 factual recall。这个现象在教师规模、KL 方向、插值系数变化下都保持稳定，没有一个常规 KD 配置在 mid-training 中对 NTP 形成完整 Pareto 改善。机制实验显示，教师熵与数据类型、监督质量、学生已掌握知识之间有系统相关性：教师在 procedural data 上熵更低，学生在 pre-training 中也更早获得与低熵相关的事实，高熵未学事实在 mid-training 阶段被 KD 弱化。Switch Distillation 缓解了这个问题。使用 OLMo-2 7B Instruct teacher 时，相比 NTP，平均 reasoning 提升 71%，knowledge 与 commonsense 提升 19%，factual recall 只下降约 1 个百分点。经过 recall 风险；以及能否把熵路由扩展为连续权重，而不是二值切换。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks whether logit-based knowledge distillation remains beneficial when moved from pre-training into mid-training. The answer is stage-dependent. Standard forward-KL distillation improves both reasoning and factual recall during pre-training, but during mid-training it produces a reasoning-recall tradeoff: reasoning improves, while factual recall acquisition slows. The failure mode comes from the interaction between teacher confidence, the student’s partially learned knowledge state, and the way KL distillation weakens the hard next-token signal.","importance":"The result matters for practical base-model training because mid-training is token-limited and increasingly used as the bridge between broad pre-training and alignment. Distillation is often treated as a way to extract more signal per token from a stronger model, but this work shows that the signal is not uniformly helpful. A post-trained teacher is confident on procedural data such as math, instruction following, and structured patterns, but less confident on many open-ended factual continuations. By the start of mid-training, the student has already learned many low-entropy facts, while the remaining missing facts are disproportionately in high-entropy regions. This makes uniform KD a poor fit for factual acquisition. The proposed entropy-routed objective is a useful recipe-level idea for mid-training, cooldown, and continual pre-training: choose between teacher supervision and ground-truth supervision at the token level rather than relying on a single global KD weight.","technical_approach":"$15","experiments":"$16","reading_guide":"Read the empirical setup and Figure 1 first, since the main claim depends on a clean comparison between pre-training and mid-training under matched student scale, teacher setup, and KD strength. Gradient surgery methods mitigate this issue by constructing directions from loss-specific gradients to reduce conflict before optimizer transformation. However, even when the constructed direction is conflict-free, this property may not be preserved after optimizer transformation. Let $a_t$ denote the direction constr","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"glance","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2609_01558.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"多目标/多损失训练中做“梯度手术”后，优化器变换（动量/自适应缩放/预条件/权重衰减）会破坏“冲突已消除”的性质，导致方法失效或解释错误。","importance":"虽然以 PINN 为背景，但指出了一个更通用的训练动态问题：很多“先改梯度、再交给AdamW”等做法隐含假设梯度方向会被保留；这对LLM训练里多损失（对齐项/正则项/aux loss/MoE load balance）或梯度投影类方法的正确性有启发。","technical_approach":"形式化定义 Gradient-Update Mismatch (GUM)：梯度手术构造方向 a_t 落在由各损失梯度张成的 conflict-free cone C_t 内，并不推出优化器实际更新 u_t 仍在 C_t 内；分析原因来自历史状态、adaptive scaling、preconditioning、decoupled weight decay 等优化器机制。","experiments":"摘要未给出具体实验设置与量化结果；主要贡献看起来是概念与理论/现象阐释。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"In multi-loss training, gradient-surgery can produce a conflict-free direction, but optimizer transformations (momentum/adaptive scaling/preconditioning/weight decay) can break that property, undermining the method.","importance":"Despite being framed around PINNs, it highlights a general training-dynamics pitfall: many “modify gradients then use AdamW” approaches implicitly assume the direction is preserved. Existing work characterizes only broad trends (e.g., SFT dominates in low-data regimes), lacks a principled allocation framework, and does not examine whether the optimal ratio transfers across model sizes. We frame this problem in terms of near-optimality: rather than seeking a single optimal SFT-RL ratio, we characterize the near-optimal","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2609_01573.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作研究的是一个很实际、但过去一直缺少稳定方法的问题：在固定标注预算下，SFT 和后续 RL 阶段到底该怎么分配数据。难点不只是找一个最优比例，而是这个最优点往往不稳定，受任务、模型规模、RL 算法、评价指标影响很大；而每改一次比例，通常都要重跑整条 post-training 流水线，代价很高。作者把问题从“找唯一最优点”改写成“找近优区域”：只要某个分配能达到峰值性能的一定容忍范围内，就算可接受。核心问题因此变成：这个近优区域是否足够宽、是否随模型变大而更稳定、是否能从小模型迁移到大模型。","importance":"对预训练和后训练接口研究，这篇工作的价值不在提出新的 RL 算法，而在于给出了一个可操作的预算分配框架。它把 post-training 中常见的“网格搜索找配比”问题，转成了“先用小代理模型识别可迁移的近优区间，再在大模型上只做少量验证”。这件事的重要性有三层。第一，它说明 SFT→RL 两阶段并不是一个尖锐调参问题，很多情况下存在宽平台，这会直接影响数据采买、标注组织和训练排期。第二，它给出一个和模型 scale 相关的经验规律：模型越大，近优区域通常越宽，意味着大模型对配比误差更不敏感。第三，它把 post-training 的预算优化问题，和 pretraining 里常见的 scaling-law / mixture optimization 思路接上了：不再执着于单点最优，而是研究稳定区域、可迁移结构和低成本代理实验。这对做大规模训练 recipe 的人很有启发。","technical_approach":"方法上，作者先形式化了固定预算 B 下的两阶段分配问题。设比例 r 分给 SFT，1-r 分给 RL，性能记为 P(N, r, B; τ)。传统做法是找 r* = argmax_r P，但作者认为这个目标病态，因为 post-training 曲线常常在峰值附近形成平台，单点最优既不稳也不值得追。于是他们定义 ε-near-optimal region：所有能达到最优性能的 1-ε 以上的 r 所组成的集合。这个定义有两个好处。其一，它更贴近工程决策，因为真实系统里 1% 到 5% 的性能差异，往往不足以支撑一次昂贵重训。其二，它允许讨论“区域宽度”和“区域迁移性”，而不只是某个点。\n\n全文的技术主线有三部分。第一部分是区域刻画。作者在不同任务、模型家族、RL 方法上扫描 r，观察性能曲线的形状，并用不同 ε 阈值计算近优区间宽度。结论是即便 ε 很小，区间也常常不窄，说明 SFT/RL 配比不是高灵敏度旋钮。第二部分是尺度迁移。作者比较小模型和大模型上的近优区间，关注的不是最优点是否完全一致，而是小模型识别出的近优区间，是否能覆盖大模型上的高性能解。这个视角比比较 argmax 更合理，因为单点最优容易受噪声和训练波动影响。第三部分是成本非对称扩展。前面默认 SFT 与 RL 样本单位成本相近，后面进一步讨论当 SFT 标注更贵或 RL 偏好数据更贵时，最优分配如何偏移。作者发现当 SFT 相对更贵时，可接受区域会进一步变宽，意味着工程上更容易选择偏向 RL 的方案而不明显损失性能。\n\n和已有工作相比，区别主要有三点。Raghavendra 等工作更多是在描述低数据区 SFT 更重要、规模上来后 preference/RL 更有利的总体趋势，但没有给出一个可迁移的分配框架，也没有把“近优而非精确最优”作为核心对象。数据 mixture 优化和 scaling-law 外推工作通常处理的是单阶段训练，或者是预训练语料配比，不涉及 SFT→RL 这种顺序依赖的两阶段耦合问题。这里的创新不在模型结构，而在问题重述、评价对象选择，以及把小模型代理实验变成大模型预算决策工具。","experiments":"从全文给出的结果看，最关键的实验结论有四个。第一，性能-配比曲线在峰值附近普遍存在平台，而不是尖峰。作者用 2% 到 10% 的相对容忍度定义近优区域，发现即便容忍度很小，区域也已经相当宽。这说明很多配比都能达到接近最优的效果，工程上没必要执着于单个比例。第二，随着模型规模增大，近优区域通常变宽。也就是说，大模型对 SFT/RL 配比更鲁棒，小模型上看起来需要精调的比例，到大模型上往往变成一段都可接受。第三，小模型到大模型的迁移性是成立的，但成立方式不是“最优点完全一致”，而是“小模型找到的近优区间，能较稳定地覆盖大模型上的高性能配比”。这是全文最有用的经验结论。第四，这个现象在不同任务、不同模型家族、以及 off-policy / on-policy RL 设置下都能复现，说明它不是某个算法偶然带来的。\n\n文中还给了一个很实用的成本量级比较：按他们测得的训练时间和 L40S 云价，单样本训练计算成本大约在 2e-5 美元到 3e-4 美元之间；相比人工标注 0.5 到 1 美元/样本，低了 3 到 5 个数量级。即便和 1e-3 美元/样本的廉价合成标注相比，训练算力成本通常也仍低于标注成本。这组数字支撑了他们把“标注预算”而不是“训练 FLOPs”作为一阶优化对象。摘要里没有完整列出所有模型尺寸、任务名和具体峰值差值，正文的重点也不是追求某个 benchmark 上的绝对分数，而是展示区域宽度、尺度变化和迁移覆盖率这些结构性结果。读的时候应把注意力放在曲线形状和区间重叠，而不是单个最高点。","reading_guide":"建议先看 Introduction 和 Preliminaries，确认作者为什么放弃“找最优点”而改用“近优区域”。如果这个问题设定接受了，后面的实验会很顺。精读时重点看三类图表。第一类是不同 r 下的性能曲线，观察峰值附近是否真的是平台，以及平台宽度如何随 ε 变化。第二类是小模型与大模型的区间对照图，重点不是两个最优点差多少，而是小模型区间是否覆盖大模型高性能区域。第三类是成本非对称分析，理解当 SFT 和 RL 样本单位成本不同，预算轴如何重参数化，以及为什么这会改变可接受分配。\n\n如果时间有限，最值得深挖的是 Section 3.2 到 3.4。3.2 解决“为什么要看区域”；3.3 解决“为什么小模型代理实验有用”；3.4 解决“这个框架在真实标注成本不对称时还能不能用”。读实验时要特别留意作者如何定义 tolerance、如何处理训练噪声，以及他们用什么标准判断“transfer reliably”。这篇工作的核心创新不在复杂算法，而在把一个高成本调参问题变成了一个稳定的决策问题。做大模型训练 recipe 的人，应该把这个思路迁移到别的多阶段预算分配上，比如 continual pretrain 与 SFT 的配比、SFT 与 preference data 的采样策略、甚至不同 RL 信号源之间的预算分配。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies a practical but under-formalized question in LLM post-training: under a fixed annotation budget, how much should be allocated to supervised fine-tuning versus the subsequent RL stage? The difficulty is not merely finding a single best ratio. The optimum is often unstable across tasks, model scales, RL variants, and evaluation metrics, while changing the ratio usually requires rerunning the whole pipeline. The paper reframes the objective from exact optimality to near-optimality: instead of chasing one brittle optimum, it asks whether there exists a broad allocation region that stays within a small tolerance of peak performance, whether that region expands with scale, and whether it transfers from small proxy models to larger target models.","importance":"Its main contribution is methodological rather than algorithmic. It gives a usable framework for post-training budget allocation, replacing expensive full-scale grid search with a proxy-model strategy. That matters for three reasons. First, it shows that SFT-to-RL allocation is often a plateau problem, not a needle-in-a-haystack tuning problem. Second, it identifies a scale effect: larger models tend to have wider near-optimal regions, so they are less sensitive to imperfect allocation. Third, it connects post-training recipe design to ideas familiar from pretraining research, such as scaling-law thinking, mixture optimization, and robust region identification rather than exact point estimation. For researchers working on the pretrain–posttrain interface, this is a useful way to reason about annotation efficiency and recipe transfer.","technical_approach":"$17","experiments":"$18","reading_guide":"$19","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"应复现到内部 SFT/RL 预算网格；若近似最优区真的随规模变宽，会改变大模型后训练 sweep 的做法。","en":"Worth reproducing on internal SFT/RL budget grids; if the near-optimal region really widens with scale, large-model post-training sweeps should change."},"metadata":{"institutions":["MIT CSAIL","Massachusetts Institute of Technology","Affiliation:CSAIL, Massachusetts Institute of Technology"],"authors":["Jingtan Wang","Arun Verma","Xiaoqiang Lin","Zhengyuan Liu","Nancy F.
